## Anatomy

### Q1 — Zygote with zona pellucida reaches uterine *(2016)*

Zygote with zona pellucida reaches uterine cavity by :

- A. 2 days
- B. 4 days
- C. 5 days
- D. 6 days

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 4 days**

</details>

### Q2 — Auerbachs plexus is present in the *(2016)*

Auerbachs plexus is present in the -

- A. Colon
- B. Esophagus
- C. Stomach
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All of the above**

</details>

### Q3 — Coracohumeral ligament inserts on *(2016)*

Coracohumeral ligament inserts on?

- A. Greater tuberosity
- B. Lesser and greater tuberosities
- C. Anatomical neck of humerus
- D. Bicepetal groove

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Lesser and greater tuberosities**

</details>

### Q4 — Longest cutaneous nerve in body *(2016)*

Longest cutaneous nerve in body ?

- A. Lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh
- B. Medial cutaneous nerve of thigh
- C. Saphenous nerve
- D. Sural nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Saphenous nerve**

</details>

### Q5 — Apex at of the lung lies *(2016)*

Apex at of the lung lies at what level?

- A. Above the clavicle
- B. Below the clavicle
- C. At the level of the clavicle
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Above the clavicle**

</details>

### Q6 — muscle causes opening of the upper *(2016)*

Which muscle causes opening of the upper end of esophagus?

- A. Epiglottis
- B. Thyropharungeus
- C. Stylopharyngeus
- D. Cricopharyngeus of inferior constrictor

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Cricopharyngeus of inferior constrictor**

</details>

### Q7 — are true about cutaneous anthrax except *(2016)*

All are true about cutaneous anthrax except ?

- A. Extremely painful
- B. The whole area is congested and edematous
- C. Central crustation with black eschar
- D. Satellite nodule around inguinal region

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Extremely painful**

</details>

### Q8 — Vascular sign of Narath is noticed *(2016)*

Vascular sign of Narath is noticed in

- A. Fracture neck of femur
- B. Perthes disease
- C. Posterior dislocation of hip
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Posterior dislocation of hip**

</details>

---

---
### Q9 — Tongue muscles are derived from *(2016)*

Tongue muscles are derived from

- A. Lateral plate mesoderm
- B. Occipital myotome
- C. Intermediate mesoderm
- D. Cervical myotome

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Occipital myotome** — **B. Occipital myotome** — Tongue musculature (intrinsic and extrinsic) migrates from occipital somites/myotomes into the pharyngeal floor; hence innervated by CN XII (hypoglossal).

</details>

---

---
### Q10 — Sensory loss in first web space *(2016)*

Sensory loss in first web space (on dorsal side). If lesion is low

- A. Type 1
- B. Type 2
- C. Teres major
- D. Supraspinatus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Type 2** — **B. Type 2** — Dorsal first web space sensation is supplied by the deep peroneal nerve (L4-L5); Type 2 low peroneal lesion affects the deep branch causing this sensory loss without foot drop.

</details>

---

---
### Q11 — Optic vesicle is derived from - *(2016)*

Optic vesicle is derived from -

- A. Endoderm
- B. Mesoderm
- C. Neuroectoderm
- D. Surface Ectoderm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Neuroectoderm** — **C. Neuroectoderm** — Optic vesicle is an evagination of the diencephalon (forebrain neuroectoderm); it induces the overlying surface ectoderm to form the lens placode.

</details>

---

---
### Q12 — Part of neural tube from which *(2016)*

Part of neural tube from which corpus callosum develops

- A. Basal lamina
- B. Alar lamina
- C. Lamina terminalis
- D. Basal plate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lamina terminalis** — **C. Lamina terminalis** — Corpus callosum develops from the commissural plate within the lamina terminalis (rostral wall of the third ventricle); agenesis results in Probst bundles.

</details>

---

---
### Q13 — Epithelial lining of urinary bladder *(2016)*

Epithelial lining of urinary bladder ?

- A. Squamous
- B. Transitional
- C. Cuboidal
- D. Columnar

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Transitional** — **B. Transitional** — Urinary bladder is lined by transitional epithelium (urothelium), which allows distension; extends from renal pelvis to urethra proximally.

</details>

---

---
### Q14 — Female urethra develops from - *(2016)*

Female urethra develops from -

- A. Urogenital sinus
- B. Mesonephric duct
- C. Ureteric bud
- D. Metanephric Blastema

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Urogenital sinus** — **A. Urogenital sinus** — The entire female urethra (shorter, ~4 cm) develops from the vesical part of the urogenital sinus; homologous to the prostatic and membranous urethra in males.

</details>

---

---
### Q15 — Kidney parenchyma is derived from - *(2016)*

Kidney parenchyma is derived from -

- A. Ureteric bud
- B. Mesonephros
- C. Metanephros
- D. Paramesonephros

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Metanephros** — **C. Metanephros** — Metanephric mesoderm (metanephric blastema) differentiates into the secretory units (nephrons) of the permanent kidney; the ureteric bud forms the collecting system.

</details>

---

---
### Q16 — Facial nerve is a derivative of *(2016)*

Facial nerve is a derivative of which of the following branchial arch?

- A. First arch
- B. Second arch
- C. Third arch
- D. Fourth arch

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Second arch** — **B. Second arch** — CN VII (facial nerve) is the nerve of the 2nd pharyngeal (Reichert's) arch; supplies all muscles of facial expression derived from this arch.

</details>

---

---
### Q17 — Leptotene and pachytene are stages ofwhich *(2016)*

Leptotene and pachytene are stages ofwhich phases of meiosis -

- A. Prophase I
- B. Metaphase I
- C. Anaphase II
- D. Telophase II

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Prophase I** — **A. Prophase I** — Prophase I of meiosis has five substages: leptotene, zygotene, pachytene, diplotene, and diakinesis; synapsis and crossing-over occur during pachytene.

</details>

---

---
### Q18 — Crypta magna develops from which pouch *(2016)*

Crypta magna develops from which pouch?

- A. 1st
- B. 2nd
- C. 3rd
- D. 4th

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 2nd** — **B. 2nd** — Crypta magna (tonsillar fossa) develops from the 2nd pharyngeal pouch; the palatine tonsil forms from lymphoid tissue proliferating into this cleft.

</details>

---

---
### Q19 — of the following is a traction *(2016)*

Which of the following is a traction epiphysis?

- A. Distal Radius
- B. Mastoid process
- C. Tibial Condyles
- D. Coracoid Process

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Mastoid process** — **B. Mastoid process** — Traction epiphyses (apophyses) develop at tendon/ligament insertions; the mastoid process is pulled by the sternocleidomastoid, making it a traction epiphysis.

</details>

---

---
### Q20 — of the following are true about *(2016)*

All of the following are true about thymus except?

- A. The cortical portion is mainly composed of lymphocytes
- B. The medulla contains Hassall's Corpuscles
- C. It is derived from the fourth Pharyngeal pouch
- D. It undergoes atrophy puberty onwards

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. It is derived from the fourth Pharyngeal pouch** — **C. It is derived from the fourth Pharyngeal pouch** — Thymus derives from the 3rd pharyngeal pouch (not 4th); the 4th pouch gives rise to the superior parathyroid glands.

</details>

---

---
### Q21 — is the most abundant cartilage- *(2016)*

Which is the most abundant cartilage-

- A. Hyaline cartilage
- B. Elastic cartilage
- C. Fibrocartilage
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hyaline cartilage** — **A. Hyaline cartilage** — Hyaline cartilage is the most abundant type; found in articular surfaces, costal cartilages, tracheal rings, and forms the embryonic skeleton.

</details>

---

---
### Q22 — is the lining of the lacrimal *(2016)*

What is the lining of the lacrimal gland alveoli?

- A. Ciliated columnar cells
- B. Pyramidal cells
- C. Non keratinizing squamous epithelium
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Pyramidal cells** — **B. Pyramidal cells** — Lacrimal gland alveoli are lined by pyramidal (columnar) secretory cells with surrounding myoepithelial cells; structure similar to other serous exocrine glands.

</details>

---

---
### Q23 — type of muscles are medial two *(2016)*

What type of muscles are medial two lumbricals?

- A. Unipennate
- B. Bipennate
- C. Multipennate
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Bipennate** — **B. Bipennate** — Medial two (3rd and 4th) lumbricals arise from two adjacent FDP tendons (bipennate); lateral two arise from a single tendon (unipennate); all are innervated by the ulnar nerve.

</details>

---

---
### Q24 — Ansa nephroni is lined by *(2016)*

Ansa nephroni is lined by ?

- A. Columnar
- B. Squamous epithelium
- C. Cuboidal and columnar epithelium
- D. Stratified squamous epithelium

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Squamous epithelium** — **B. Squamous epithelium** — The loop of Henle (ansa nephroni), especially the thin descending and thin ascending limbs, is lined by flat squamous epithelium facilitating passive water/solute exchange.

</details>

---

---
### Q25 — is the level of the spine *(2016)*

What is the level of the spine of scapula?

- A. T7
- B. T I 0
- C. T4
- D. T2

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. T4** — **C. T4** — The spine of scapula lies at the level of T4 vertebral spine (T3 spinous process at the medial end); the inferior angle of scapula corresponds to T7.

</details>

---

---
### Q26 — of the following muscles carries out *(2016)*

Which of the following muscles carries out shoulder abduction from 15 to 90 degrees?

- A. Suprapinatus
- B. Trapezius
- C. Deltoid
- D. Serratus Anterior

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Deltoid** — **C. Deltoid** — Deltoid is the prime mover of shoulder abduction from 15–90°; supraspinatus initiates first 15° and stabilizes the joint; beyond 90° trapezius and serratus anterior rotate scapula.

</details>

---

---
### Q27 — muscle acting on the thumb has *(2016)*

Which muscle acting on the thumb has dual nerve supply?

- A. Flexor Pollicis Longus
- B. Flexor Pollicis brevis
- C. Adductor Pollicis
- D. Opponens Pollicis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Flexor Pollicis brevis** — **B. Flexor Pollicis brevis** — Flexor pollicis brevis has a superficial head (median nerve) and deep head (ulnar nerve/deep branch); only thumb muscle with consistent dual nerve supply.

</details>

---

---
### Q28 — is Wartenbergs sign *(2016)*

What is Wartenberg's sign?

- A. Inability to maintain Intrinsic plus position
- B. Inability to adduct small finger against the ring finger
- C. Inability to grasp a book between the thumb and index finger
- D. Inability to move the middle finger sideways

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Inability to adduct small finger against the ring finger** — **B. Inability to adduct small finger against the ring finger** — Wartenberg's sign indicates ulnar nerve palsy; interossei (ulnar) adduct fingers; loss leaves the little finger abducted at rest.

</details>

---

---
### Q29 — Sensory region of the ulnar nerve *(2016)*

Sensory region of the ulnar nerve is?

- A. Tip of little finger
- B. Tip of index finger
- C. 1st web space
- D. Lateral upper aspect of arm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Tip of little finger** — **A. Tip of little finger** — Ulnar nerve (C8-T1) provides cutaneous sensation to the little finger and medial half of ring finger; tip of little finger is the classic autonomous zone for testing.

</details>

---

---
### Q30 — Pulp of the index finger is *(2016)*

Pulp of the index finger is supplied by

- A. Median nerve
- B. Radial nerve
- C. Ulnar nerve
- D. Axillary nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Median nerve** — **A. Median nerve** — The median nerve (C6-C8) supplies the palmar surface and pulp of the lateral 3.5 fingers (thumb, index, middle, lateral half of ring); index fingertip is its autonomous zone.

</details>

---

---
### Q31 — Low radial nerve just after spiral *(2016)*

Low radial nerve [just after spiral groove] palsy does not produce ?

- A. Loss of wrist extention
- B. Loss of elbow extention
- C. Loss of finger extention
- D. Loss of thumb extention

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Loss of elbow extention** — **B. Loss of elbow extension** — Triceps (elbow extension) is innervated proximal to the spiral groove; a low radial nerve lesion after the groove spares triceps but paralyzes wrist and finger extensors.

</details>

---

---
### Q32 — Structure passing deep to flexor retinaculum *(2016)*

Structure passing deep to flexor retinaculum at wrist:

- A. Ulnar nerve
- B. Median nerve
- C. Radial nerve
- D. Ulnar artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Median nerve** — **B. Median nerve** — The carpal tunnel (deep to flexor retinaculum) transmits the median nerve and 9 flexor tendons; ulnar nerve and artery pass superficial in Guyon's canal.

</details>

---

---
### Q33 — Coracoacromial ligament resists which movements *(2016)*

Coracoacromial ligament resists which movements?

- A. Upward displacement of humeral head
- B. Abduction of shoulder
- C. Inferior displacement of humerus
- D. External rotation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Upward displacement of humeral head** — **A. Upward displacement of humeral head** — The coracoacromial arch (coracoacromial ligament + acromion) prevents superior displacement of the humeral head during deltoid contraction and arm elevation.

</details>

---

---
### Q34 — is the action of anconeus *(2016)*

What is the action of anconeus?

- A. Primary elbow extensor
- B. Assists Extension of elbow
- C. Wrist extension
- D. Thumb Abduction

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Assists Extension of elbow** — **B. Assists Extension of elbow** — Anconeus is a small triangular muscle on the posterior lateral elbow that assists triceps in elbow extension and abducts the ulna during pronation.

</details>

---

---
### Q35 — muscle helps in climbing a tree *(2016)*

Which muscle helps in climbing a tree ?

- A. Latissimus Dorsi
- B. Rhomboideus
- C. Trapezius
- D. Levator scapulae

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Latissimus Dorsi** — **A. Latissimus Dorsi** — Latissimus dorsi (thoracodorsal nerve) adducts, extends, and medially rotates the humerus; climbing requires pulling the trunk upward toward the fixed upper limb.

</details>

---

---
### Q36 — Posterior interosseus artery is a branch *(2016)*

Posterior interosseus artery is a branch of ?

- A. Common interosseus artery
- B. Radial artery
- C. Median artery
- D. Brachial artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Common interosseus artery** — **A. Common interosseus artery** — The common interosseous artery arises from the ulnar artery and immediately divides into anterior and posterior interosseous arteries supplying the deep forearm compartments.

</details>

---

---
### Q37 — muscle protects the brachial plexus in *(2016)*

Which muscle protects the brachial plexus in case of clavicle fractures?

- A. Subclavius
- B. Supraspinatus
- C. Subscapularius
- D. Teres Minor

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Subclavius** — **A. Subclavius** — Subclavius muscle lies between the clavicle and first rib; it cushions the brachial plexus and subclavian vessels from injury during mid-shaft clavicle fractures.

</details>

---

---
### Q38 — of the following is true about *(2016)*

Which of the following is true about deep palmar arch?

- A. Mainly formed by the radial artery
- B. Ulnar artery has no contribution to it
- C. It gives off 5 perforating branches
- D. It does not anastomose with the superficial palmar arch

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Mainly formed by the radial artery** — **A. Mainly formed by the radial artery** — The deep palmar arch is predominantly formed by the radial artery (enters palm via anatomical snuffbox) with a small contribution from the deep branch of the ulnar artery.

</details>

---

---
### Q39 — part of scapula can be palpated *(2016)*

Which part of scapula can be palpated in the infraclavicular fossa?

- A. Coracoid process
- B. Spine of scapula
- C. Inferior angle
- D. Supraspinous fossa

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Coracoid process** — **A. Coracoid process** — The coracoid process projects anterolaterally from the scapular neck and is palpable in the infraclavicular fossa, 1 inch below the clavicle at the deltopectoral groove.

</details>

---

---
### Q40 — Flexor carpi radialis inserts into *(2016)*

Flexor carpi radialis inserts into ?

- A. Base of 5th metatarsal
- B. Base of 2nd and 3rd metacarpal
- C. Scaphoid and trapezium
- D. Capitate and hamate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Base of 2nd and 3rd metacarpal** — **B. Base of 2nd and 3rd metacarpal** — Flexor carpi radialis inserts primarily into the base of the 2nd metacarpal (and a slip to 3rd); it flexes and abducts the wrist, innervated by the median nerve.

</details>

---

---
### Q41 — is true about lateral tibial condyle *(2016)*

What is true about lateral tibial condyle ?

- A. Iliotibial tract is attached to the lateral condyle of tibia
- B. Ligamentum patellae inserts on it
- C. Medial collateral ligament is attached to it
- D. Semimembranosus is attached to it

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Iliotibial tract is attached to the lateral condyle of tibia** — **A. Iliotibial tract is attached to the lateral condyle of tibia** — The iliotibial tract inserts on Gerdy's tubercle on the lateral tibial condyle; injury causes IT band syndrome in runners.

</details>

---

---
### Q42 — Weakness of extensor Hallucis longus is *(2016)*

Weakness of extensor Hallucis longus is due to which nerve root mainly?

- A. L5
- B. L4
- C. S1
- D. S2

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. L5** — **A. L5** — Extensor hallucis longus is primarily innervated by the deep peroneal nerve (L4-L5); L5 root compression (e.g., disc prolapse at L4-L5) causes weakness of great toe extension.

</details>

---

---
### Q43 — tendon is lodged in the groove *(2016)*

Which tendon is lodged in the groove on posterior surface of lateral malleolus?

- A. Peroneus longus
- B. Tibialis anterior
- C. Tibialis posterior
- D. Flexor Hallucis Longus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Peroneus longus** — **A. Peroneus longus** — The peroneus longus tendon (along with peroneus brevis) runs in the groove posterior to the lateral malleolus, held by the superior peroneal retinaculum.

</details>

---

---
### Q44 — of the following are true about *(2016)*

All of the following are true about tibialis anterior except ?

- A. It is supplied by the superficial peroneal nerve
- B. It dorsiflexes the foot
- C. It is closely related to the anterior tibial vessels
- D. It inserts on the medial cuneiform

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. It is supplied by the superficial peroneal nerve** — **A. It is supplied by the superficial peroneal nerve** — Tibialis anterior is supplied by the deep peroneal nerve (not superficial); the superficial peroneal nerve supplies peroneus longus and brevis.

</details>

---

---
### Q45 — Structures passing through sacral hiatus are *(2016)*

Structures passing through sacral hiatus are ?

- A. S4 nerve root
- B. S2 nerve root
- C. S3 nerve root
- D. S5 nerve root

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. S5 nerve root** — **D. S5 nerve root** — The sacral hiatus (failed fusion of S4-S5 laminae) transmits the S5 nerve roots, coccygeal nerve, and filum terminale; used for caudal epidural anaesthesia.

</details>

---

---
### Q46 — Lateral border of the foot receives *(2016)*

Lateral border of the foot receives its sensory supply from ?

- A. Saphenous nerve
- B. Sural nerve
- C. Deep peroneal nerve
- D. Sciatic nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Sural nerve** — **B. Sural nerve** — The sural nerve (S1-S2), formed from branches of the tibial and common peroneal nerves, supplies the lateral border and heel of the foot; its autonomous zone is the lateral foot.

</details>

---

---
### Q47 — of the following is included in *(2016)*

All of the following is included in chest wall except?

- A. Ribs
- B. Thoracic Vertebrae
- C. Sternum
- D. Lumbar vertebrae

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Lumbar vertebrae** — **D. Lumbar vertebrae** — The thoracic cage consists of 12 thoracic vertebrae, 12 pairs of ribs, and the sternum; lumbar vertebrae belong to the abdominal region, not the chest wall.

</details>

---

---
### Q48 — Respiratory bronchioles are formed from *(2016)*

Respiratory bronchioles are formed from ?

- A. Principal bronchus
- B. Terminal bronchioles
- C. Tertiary Bronchus
- D. Lobar bronchioles

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Terminal bronchioles** — **B. Terminal bronchioles** — Terminal bronchioles (non-respiratory) give rise to respiratory bronchioles, which are the first airways with alveoli in their walls marking the beginning of the respiratory zone.

</details>

---

---
### Q49 — cricopharyngeal sphincter is how far from *(2016)*

The cricopharyngeal sphincter is how far from the central incisor?

- A. 15cm
- B. 25cm
- C. 40cm
- D. 50cm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 15cm** — **A. 15cm** — The cricopharyngeal sphincter (pharyngoesophageal junction, upper oesophageal sphincter) is approximately 15 cm from the central incisor teeth; a key landmark during endoscopy.

</details>

---

---
### Q50 — of the following structures is related *(2016)*

Which of the following structures is related to the esophagus 22.5cm from the incisor teeth?

- A. Arch of aorta
- B. Right principal broncus
- C. Thoracic Duct
- D. Azygous Vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Arch of aorta** — **A. Arch of aorta** — The arch of aorta crosses the left side of the oesophagus at approximately 22.5 cm from the incisor teeth; this is a classic constriction point of the oesophagus.

</details>

---

---
### Q51 — Thoracic duct opens into systemic circulation *(2016)*

Thoracic duct opens into systemic circulation at?

- A. junction of SVC and left brachiocephalic vein
- B. Junction of left internal jugular and left subclavian vein
- C. Directly into coronary sinus
- D. Into azygous vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Junction of left internal jugular and left subclavian vein** — **B. Junction of left internal jugular and left subclavian vein** — The thoracic duct drains at the left venous angle (jugulosubclavian junction); injury here causes left chylothorax.

</details>

---

---
### Q52 — Sympathetic supply of the heart is *(2016)*

Sympathetic supply of the heart is from ?

- A. Vagus
- B. Thoracic sympathetic fibres [T1 to T5]
- C. Lumbar sympathetic fibres
- D. Cervical ganglion

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Thoracic sympathetic fibres [T1 to T5]** — **B. Thoracic sympathetic fibres [T1 to T5]** — Cardiac sympathetic innervation originates from T1-T5 lateral horn neurons; postganglionic fibres pass via cervical and thoracic cardiac nerves to the cardiac plexus.

</details>

---

---
### Q53 — of the following are cusps of *(2016)*

Which of the following are cusps of the aortic valves?

- A. Left, right and Anterior
- B. Anterior, Right and Posterior
- C. Posterior, Left and Right
- D. Anterior, Posterior and Left

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Posterior, Left and Right** — **C. Posterior, Left and Right** — The aortic valve has three semilunar cusps: right (anterior), left, and posterior (non-coronary); right and left cusps have the coronary ostia above them.

</details>

---

---
### Q54 — of the following passes posterior to *(2016)*

Which of the following passes posterior to the hilum of the lung?

- A. Vagus
- B. Phrenic nerve
- C. SVC
- D. Right atrium

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Vagus** — **A. Vagus** — The vagus nerve passes posterior to the lung hilum on both sides; the phrenic nerve passes anterior to the hilum — a key anatomical distinction for thoracic surgery.

</details>

---

---
### Q55 — is the level of the pulmonary *(2016)*

What is the level of the pulmonary valve?

- A. 3rd intercostal space
- B. 4th costal cartilage
- C. 3rd costal cartilage
- D. 2nd intercostal space

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 3rd costal cartilage** — **C. 3rd costal cartilage** — The pulmonary valve lies at the level of the 3rd left costal cartilage; best auscultated in the 2nd left intercostal space (pulmonary area).

</details>

---

---
### Q56 — Lower limit of the inferior border *(2016)*

Lower limit of the inferior border of the lung in the midaxillary line is ?

- A. 6th rib
- B. 8th rib
- C. 10th rib
- D. 12th rib

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 8th rib** — **B. 8th rib** — The inferior lung border crosses the 6th rib at the midclavicular line, 8th rib at the midaxillary line, and 10th rib at the paravertebral line; the pleural reflection is 2 ribs lower.

</details>

---

---
### Q57 — is a typical intercostal nerve *(2016)*

Which is a typical intercostal nerve?

- A. First
- B. Second
- C. Third
- D. Seventh

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Third** — **C. Third** — Intercostal nerves 3–6 are typical (run only in the intercostal space); the 1st, 2nd, 7th–11th, and 12th (subcostal) are atypical due to communications or unusual courses.

</details>

---
### Q58 — Great cardiac vein lies in *(2016)*

Great cardiac vein lies in ?

- A. Tricuspid valve
- B. Anterior interventricular sulcus
- C. Posterior interventricular sulcus
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Anterior interventricular sulcus** — **B. Anterior interventricular sulcus** — The great cardiac vein ascends in the anterior interventricular groove alongside the left anterior descending artery, then drains into the coronary sinus.

</details>

---
### Q59 — Diaphragm is supplied by *(2016)*

Diaphragm is supplied by ?

- A. Phrenic nerve
- B. C2,C3,C4 Roots
- C. Thoracodorsal nerve
- D. Long thoracic nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Phrenic nerve** — **A. Phrenic nerve** — The phrenic nerve (C3, C4, C5 — 'keeps the diaphragm alive') provides both motor and sensory (central) supply; intercostal nerves supply only peripheral sensory fibers.

</details>

---
### Q60 — Midpoint between suprasternal notch and pubic *(2016)*

Midpoint between suprasternal notch and pubic symphyses passes through which plane?

- A. Transpyloric plane
- B. Transtubercular plane
- C. Trnasxiphoid plane
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Transpyloric plane** — **A. Transpyloric plane** — The transpyloric plane (L1) lies at the midpoint between the suprasternal notch and pubic symphysis; it passes through the pylorus, L1 vertebra, and fundus of gallbladder.

</details>

---
### Q61 — Muscle lying between anterior and middle *(2016)*

Muscle lying between anterior and middle layer of thoracolumbar fascia is ?

- A. Psoas major
- B. Quadratus Lumborum
- C. Obdurator internus
- D. External oblique

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Quadratus Lumborum** — **B. Quadratus Lumborum** — Quadratus lumborum lies between the anterior and middle layers of thoracolumbar fascia; psoas major lies anterior to the anterior layer.

</details>

---
### Q62 — Anterior Rectus Sheath just above pubic *(2016)*

Anterior Rectus Sheath just above pubic symphysis is formed by ?

- A. External Oblique Aponeurosis
- B. The aponeurosis of three muscles including External Oblique,
- C. Linea Alba
- D. Internal Oblique only

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. The aponeurosis of three muscles including External Oblique,** — **B. The aponeurosis of three muscles including External Oblique** — Below the arcuate line, all three flat muscle aponeuroses (external oblique, internal oblique, transversus) pass anterior to rectus abdominis, forming the anterior sheath.

</details>

### Q63 — Right suprarenal vein drains into *(2016)*

Right suprarenal vein drains into ?

- A. Inferior vena cava
- B. Right renal vein
- C. Left renal vein
- D. Accessory Hemiazygous vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Inferior vena cava** — **A. Inferior vena cava** — The right suprarenal (adrenal) vein drains directly into the IVC; the left suprarenal vein drains into the left renal vein — an important surgical distinction during adrenalectomy.

</details>

---
### Q64 — of the following is not derived *(2016)*

Which of the following is not derived from the external oblique aponeurosis?

- A. Inguinal Ligament
- B. Lacunar ligament
- C. Line Semilunaris
- D. Pectineal Ligament

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Line Semilunaris** — **C. Line Semilunaris** — Linea semilunaris (Spigelian line) is the lateral edge of the rectus sheath formed by all three flat muscle aponeuroses; the inguinal, lacunar, and pectineal ligaments derive from the external oblique aponeurosis.

</details>

---

---
### Q65 — Stomach is supplied by *(2016)*

Stomach is supplied by ?

- A. Coeliac trunk
- B. Splenic artery
- C. Gastroduodenal artery
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All of the above** — **D. All of the above** — Stomach receives blood from all branches of the coeliac trunk: left gastric (direct), right gastric and gastroduodenal (hepatic), left and right gastroepiploic, and short gastric (splenic) arteries.

</details>

---

---
### Q66 — is the number of layers in *(2016)*

What is the number of layers in greater omentum?

- A. 1
- B. 2
- C. 3
- D. 4

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 4** — **D. 4** — The greater omentum consists of 4 peritoneal layers (two folds of peritoneum, each a double layer); it descends from the greater curvature of the stomach and reflects back to the transverse colon.

</details>

---

---
### Q67 — Stomach wall is mainly drained by *(2016)*

Stomach wall is mainly drained by all lymphnodes except?

- A. Pyloric nodes
- B. Short gastric vessel nodal group
- C. Right gastroepiploic nodes
- D. Inguinal nodes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Inguinal nodes** — **D. Inguinal nodes** — Stomach lymphatics drain to nodes along its curvatures (gastric, gastroepiploic, pyloric) then to coeliac nodes; inguinal nodes drain the lower limb and perineum, not the stomach.

</details>

---

---
### Q68 — lymph of stomach drains into *(2016)*

All lymph of stomach drains into ?

- A. Pyloric nodes
- B. Short gastric vessel nodal group
- C. Right gastroepiploic nodes
- D. Coeliac nodes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Coeliac nodes** — **D. Coeliac nodes** — All gastric lymph ultimately converges at the coeliac lymph nodes (pre-aortic group at the coeliac trunk origin), which drain to the cisterna chyli.

</details>

---
### Q69 — Internal anal Sphincter is formed by *(2016)*

Internal anal Sphincter is formed by ?

- B. Circular muscles from lower rectum
- C. Longitudinal Involuntary muscles
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Circular muscles from lower rectum** — **B. Circular muscles from lower rectum** — The internal anal sphincter is a thickened continuation of the inner circular smooth muscle of the rectum; it is involuntary, supplied by autonomic nerves.

</details>

---
### Q70 — of the following organs are in *(2016)*

All of the following organs are in direct contact with the spleen except?

- B. Stomach
- C. Left kidney
- D. Colon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Duodenum** — **A. Duodenum** — The spleen is in direct contact with the stomach, left kidney, left colic flexure, and tail of pancreas; the duodenum is on the right side and has no direct splenic contact.

</details>

---
### Q71 — Glans penis is a continuation of *(2016)*

Glans penis is a continuation of -

- A. Corpus spongiosum
- B. Ischiocavernosus
- C. Corpora Cavernosa
- D. Puborectalis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Corpus spongiosum** — **A. Corpus spongiosum** — The glans penis is the expanded distal end of the corpus spongiosum, which surrounds the urethra; the two corpora cavernosa terminate proximal to the glans.

</details>

---
### Q72 — Cremastric muscle is formed from *(2016)*

Cremastric muscle is formed from ?

- A. Fascia from internal oblique
- B. Fascia from external oblique
- C. Fascia from rectus abdominis
- D. Fascia from transversus abdominis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Fascia from internal oblique** — **A. Fascia from internal oblique** — Cremasteric muscle and fascia are derived from the lowermost fibres of the internal oblique (and transversus abdominis); the cremaster reflex is mediated by the genitofemoral nerve (L1-L2).

</details>

### Q73 — Kidney is covered by what fascia *(2016)*

Kidney is covered by what fascia?

- B. Buck's Fascia
- C. Gerota's Fascia
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Gerota's Fascia** — **C. Gerota's Fascia** — Gerota's fascia (renal/perirenal fascia) encloses the kidney and perirenal fat; it is open inferiorly and fused superiorly, important in staging renal tumours.

</details>

### Q74 — Narrowest part of ureter is *(2016)*

Narrowest part of ureter is ?

- B. Crossing by gonadal vessels

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Vesicouretric junction** — **C. Vesicouretric junction** — The vesicoureteric junction (VUJ) is the narrowest part of the ureter (~2–3 mm); the most common site for renal calculi to lodge, causing obstructive renal colic.

</details>

### Q75 — is the total length of the *(2016)*

What is the total length of the colon?

**B. 1.5 metres** — **B. 1.5 metres** — The large intestine (colon) is approximately 1.5 m (5 feet) long, extending from the ileocaecal valve to the rectum; distinguished from small intestine by haustrations and taeniae coli.

</details>

### Q76 — Submandibular lymphnodes drain the following areas *(2016)*

Submandibular lymphnodes drain the following areas of the face except?

### Q77 — Nerve supply to the angle of *(2016)*

### Q78 — Nerve supply to the tip of *(2016)*

Nerve supply to the tip of the nose is from?

### Q79 — of the following are main branches *(2016)*

All of the following are main branches of Trigeminal nerve except ?

**D. Optic nerve** — **D. Optic nerve** — CN V (trigeminal) has three divisions: ophthalmic (V1), maxillary (V2), and mandibular (V3); CN II (optic nerve) is a separate cranial nerve carrying special sensory (vision) fibers.

</details>

### Q80 — HHH syndrome is due to defect *(2016)*

HHH syndrome is due to defect in ?

### Q81 — Soft wood exposure leads to - *(2016)*

### Q82 — of the following pairs is correct *(2016)*

Which of the following pairs is correct?

- C. Vildagliptin - SGLT2 inhibitor
- D. Voglibose - DPP4 inhibitor

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Biguanides - AMP Kinase activation** — **B. Biguanides - AMP Kinase activation** — Metformin (biguanide) activates AMPK, which inhibits hepatic gluconeogenesis and increases peripheral glucose uptake; sulfonylureas block K-ATP channels, not biguanides.

### Q83 — Panitumumab is used for which cancer *(2016)*

Panitumumab is used for which cancer?

### Q84 — common source of Diphtheria *(2016)*

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

### Q85 — Gradenigos syndrome involves all of the *(2016)*

Gradenigo's syndrome involves all of the following cranial nerves, EXCEPT:

- B. V
- C. VI
- D. VII

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. IV** — **A. IV** — Gradenigo's syndrome (petrous apicitis) involves CN V (facial pain), CN VI (lateral rectus palsy), and occasionally CN VII (facial palsy); CN IV (trochlear) is not involved in this syndrome.

### Q86 — cranial nerve is involved in Weber *(2016)*

Which cranial nerve is involved in Weber syndrome?

</details>

### Q87 — Following are the features of neuropathy *(2016)*

Following are the features of neuropathy associated with varicella- zoster infection except

- C. Shingles are distributed along motor dermatomes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Shingles are distributed along motor dermatomes** — **C. Shingles are distributed along motor dermatomes** — Shingles (herpes zoster reactivation) distributes along sensory dermatomes, not motor; varicella-zoster virus persists in sensory (dorsal root) ganglia.

</details>

### Q88 — common nerve affected in leprosy *(2016)*

Most common nerve affected in leprosy

- A. Posterior tibial

- C. Median
- D. Facial

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Posterior tibial** — **A. Posterior tibial** — The posterior tibial nerve is the most commonly affected nerve in leprosy (causing plantar anaesthesia and claw toes); the ulnar nerve is the most commonly thickened nerve.

</details>

### Q89 — Characteristic site for development of venous *(2016)*

Characteristic site for development of venous leg ulcers is

- A. Skin of gaiter region
- B. Behind lateral malleolus

- D. Medial aspect of knee

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Skin of gaiter region** — **A. Skin of gaiter region** — Venous (varicose) leg ulcers characteristically occur in the gaiter zone (lower third of leg, medial aspect above medial malleolus) due to venous hypertension and perforator incompetence.

</details>

### Q90 — In case of female commonest hernia *(2016)*

In case of female commonest hernia is ?

- B. Indirect inguinal hernia
- C. Femoral Hernia
- D. Incisional hernia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Indirect inguinal hernia** — **B. Indirect inguinal hernia** — Indirect inguinal hernia is the commonest hernia in females (as in males); femoral hernia is more common in females relative to males but is still less common than indirect inguinal overall.

</details>

### Q91 — 40 years male presents with translucent *(2016)*

40 years male presents with translucent scrotal swelling in which it is possible to get above the swelling on examination. What is the most probable diagnosis?

- A. Hydrocele
- B. Indirect inguinal hernia
- C. Varicocele
- D. Sebaceous cyst

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hydrocele** — **A. Hydrocele** — A hydrocele is a fluid collection around the testis; it transilluminates brilliantly and one can get above the swelling on palpation, distinguishing it from an inguinal hernia descending into the scrotum.

</details>

---

---
### Q92 — Scrotal swelling non reducible but disappears *(2016)*

Scrotal swelling non reducible but disappears when the child wakes up from sleep is most likely to be -

- A. Congenital hydrocele
- B. Varicocele
- C. Indirect inguinal hernia
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Congenital hydrocele** — **A. Congenital hydrocele** — Congenital (communicating) hydrocele communicates with the peritoneum via a patent processus vaginalis; fluid drains back when the child is supine/sleeping, causing the swelling to disappear overnight.

</details>

---
### Q93 — 10 years old male presents a *(2016)*

A 10 years old male presents a smooth swelling near superficial inguinal ring, which moves downwards when the testicle is pulled downwards. Diagnosis -

- A. Inguinal hernia
- B. Congenital hydrocele
- C. Encysted hydrocele of the cord
- D. Varicocele

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Encysted hydrocele of the cord** — **C. Encysted hydrocele of the cord** — Encysted hydrocele of the cord is a localised fluid collection within an isolated segment of the processus vaginalis; it moves downward with testicular traction (Landon-Morgan test).

</details>

---
### Q94 — Child while playing has sudden loss *(2016)*

Child while playing has sudden loss of consciousness and appears pale. There is no significant medical history and the child was otherwise healthy. Which of the following is the most probable

- A. Attention deficit hyperkinetic disorder
- B. Breath holding spell
- C. Autism
- D. Rett's syndrome

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Breath holding spell** — **B. Breath holding spell** — Cyanotic breath-holding spells in a healthy child triggered by emotion or pain cause sudden LOC and pallor; self-limiting, no interictal abnormality, resolves by age 5-6 years.

</details>

### Q95 — Iliotibial band contracture in patients of *(2016)*

Iliotibial band contracture in patients of poliomyelitis will lead to

- B. Flexion at hip, extention at knee
- C. Extention at hip flexion at knee
- D. Extention at hip and knee

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Flexion at hip and knee** — **A. Flexion at hip and knee** — Iliotibial band (TFL) is a hip flexor and external rotator; contracture causes flexion-abduction-external rotation at hip and flexion at knee (wind-swept deformity in polio).

</details>

### Q96 — Jumpers knee *(2016)*

Jumpers knee

- A. Apophysitis of patellar tendon as it inserts in patella

- D. Apophysitis of hamstring tendon as it inserts in tibia

</details>

### Q97 — Palpable femur head on per rectal *(2016)*

### Q98 — most common complication of intracapsular fracture *(2016)*

The most common complication of intracapsular fracture neck of femur is

- B. Osteoarthritis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

### Q99 — of the following are true regarding *(2016)*

- C. Cubitus valgus occurs

### Q100 — common complication of fracture of tibia *(2016)*

- D. Vascular injury

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Delayed union** — **C. Delayed union** — The tibia has the highest rate of delayed/non-union among long bones due to poor blood supply (no muscle in the distal third) and high incidence of open fractures.

### Q101 — Dunlop traction is a type of *(2016)*

### Q102 — of the following is true about *(2016)*

### Q103 — of the following isare features of *(2016)*

### Q104 — 33 yr old female presents with *(2016)*

- A. Local resection
- B. Amputaion

### Q105 — Proximal tibial epiphysis fuses at - *(2016)*

### Q106 — Management of displaced non comminuted intercondyl *(2016)*

### Q107 — Nerve injured in McRoberts maneuver is *(2016)*

Nerve injured in McRoberts maneuver is ?

- B. Obturator nerve

### Q108 — Shortest transverse diameter is *(2016)*

Shortest transverse diameter is ?
